<a id="expl-framework-model"></a>

# XDMOD as a SysML model

XDMOD describes itself in SysML v2, the same way a project describes its system
([*SysML v2 textual notation f... (XD-ADR-0001)*](decisions/method.html.md#XD-ADR-0001)). The model lives in `xdmod/model/`. Each diagram on this page is a
**view** of that model: a SysML `view` in `xdmod/model/views.sysml`, rendered by
`curatrix views build xdmod` into `xdmod/views/<view>.drawio.svg` ([*View definitions live in th... (XD-ADR-0085)*](decisions/method.html.md#XD-ADR-0085),
[*Views are committed as edit... (XD-ADR-0087)*](decisions/method.html.md#XD-ADR-0087)). Nobody draws these pictures by hand. To change one, change the model,
rebuild the views and commit both. `curatrix check xdmod` fails when a view no longer matches
the model ([*Generated files are committ... (XD-RULE-0033)*](rules/checks.html.md#XD-RULE-0033)).

The diagrams use the SysML v2 graphical notation where drawio has a matching shape. Each
diagram has a legend and fits a 4:3 or 16:9 page, upright or across. In short:

- A sharp box is a **definition** and a rounded box is a **usage**. The keyword in guillemets
  (`«item def»`) names the kind of element, and the short name in angle brackets (`<sys-req>`)
  comes before the name.
- A **hollow triangle** points from a specialised type to its general type.
- A **filled diamond** sits on the owner of a composite feature (a part or an item it contains).
- A **hollow diamond with an open arrow** is a reference feature. The diamond end holds the
  reference, and the label gives its name and multiplicity.
- Boxes with a decision, rule or check behind them are links: click them to open that need.

The model is checked against the configuration it describes. A test compares the need types and
link types in package `XdmodNeeds` with `needs/needs_config.toml`
([*One shared needs configuration (XD-RULE-0007)*](rules/trace.html.md#XD-RULE-0007)), so the trace diagrams below always show what sphinx-needs enforces.

## The five domains

XDMOD splits a project into five domains. Each domain has its own repo in one west workspace,
and its own need types ([*A multi-repo workspace with... (XD-ADR-0006)*](decisions/repos.html.md#XD-ADR-0006), [*An integration repo for sys... (XD-ADR-0066)*](decisions/repos.html.md#XD-ADR-0066)):

- **SYS**, systems engineering: stakeholders, system requirements and the SysML model.
- **EE**, electrical engineering: KiCad projects and prototype wiring.
- **ME**, mechanical engineering: FreeCAD parts and assemblies.
- **SW**, software: the Zephyr firmware.
- **INT**, system-level verification: system and integration tests and HIL runs. A test that
  touches the system lives here, even if it touches one domain only ([*Tests live by what they verify (XD-ADR-0104)*](decisions/traces.html.md#XD-ADR-0104)).

The domain is the first part of every ID in its repo ([*ID format DOMAIN-KIND-NNNN (XD-ADR-0071)*](decisions/traces.html.md#XD-ADR-0071)). Two more ID
prefixes are not domains: **XD** for the framework itself and **PRJ** for project decisions.

Each domain has two diagrams. The first shows its repo with the documents and design files it
holds; the `doc` compartment says where each file lives. The second shows its need types
and the trace links between them. Every need type is an `item def` whose short name is its
sphinx-needs directive (`<sys-req>`). The line `:> Requirement` names its category, its place
in the V ([Need categories]() below).

The concrete side holds the link ([*Trace rule: the concrete si... (XD-ADR-0004)*](decisions/traces.html.md#XD-ADR-0004), [*The concrete side holds the... (XD-RULE-0001)*](rules/trace.html.md#XD-RULE-0001)). The diamond sits on
the need type that holds the link, and the arrow points to the more abstract type. In the
domain diagrams the abstract types stand at the top; in the diagrams between two domains, systems
engineering stands on the left. The label on the line names the link type. `[1..*]` marks a
link that is required; the need schema in `needs_config.toml` enforces it
([*Link types are declared on ... (XD-RULE-0002)*](rules/trace.html.md#XD-RULE-0002), [*One shared needs configuration (XD-RULE-0007)*](rules/trace.html.md#XD-RULE-0007)). Some links may point to more than one need type.
The model then has one reference per target type, each `[0..*]`, and “(>= 1 in total)” says
that at least one of them is required.

### Systems engineering (SYS)

The systems repo holds the SysML model, the documents with the requirements and the architecture, and the files curatrix generates from them: requirement stubs for the model, the compact model export, the needs taken from the model, and the views ([*SysML v2 textual notation f... (XD-ADR-0001)*](decisions/method.html.md#XD-ADR-0001)).

Stakeholders raise needs; system requirements refine them. Use cases address needs, and system model elements satisfy requirements and realize use cases. A configuration selects model elements. Nothing inside SYS links to an interface: interfaces are the targets of the engineering domains and of system-level tests (next section). Features have no links; a requirement names a feature in its `where` field ([Variants](variants.html.md)).

### Electrical engineering (EE)

The EE repo holds one KiCad project per PCBA, the prototype wiring, the domain tests and the needs curatrix reads from the KiCad files ([*One EE document per PCBA an... (XD-ADR-0102)*](decisions/documents.html.md#XD-ADR-0102), [*Trace IDs live inside the C... (XD-ADR-0023)*](decisions/traces.html.md#XD-ADR-0023)).

A circuit block is part of a PCBA, and so is a connector port (`ee-port`, with its pin map from the EE model). A harness (`ee-har`) connects the ports its ends mate with; it realizes a system element and satisfies EE requirements like a PCBA. PCBAs and circuit blocks satisfy EE requirements. EE tests verify EE requirements, and test results point back to the test case they come from.

### Mechanical engineering (ME)

The ME repo holds the FreeCAD parts and assemblies, the domain tests and the needs curatrix reads from the FreeCAD files ([*FreeCAD files uncompressed,... (XD-ADR-0056)*](decisions/checks.html.md#XD-ADR-0056)).

The same pattern as EE: a part is part of an assembly, and so is a mechanical port (`me-port`, a mounting interface or a board seat); parts and assemblies satisfy ME requirements, and ME tests verify them.

### Software (SW)

The SW repo holds the Zephyr application, its tests and the software documents, from the requirements to the test report ([*SW architecture in rst in i... (XD-ADR-0069)*](decisions/method.html.md#XD-ADR-0069)).

Software has the longest chain inside one domain: requirements, architecture, detailed design and code units, which curatrix extracts from the code ([*Code units implement design... (XD-ADR-0070)*](decisions/traces.html.md#XD-ADR-0070)). Each level refines or implements the one above and satisfies the requirements.

### System-level verification (INT)

The integration repo holds the system and integration tests, the HIL setups and the HIL run records ([*An integration repo for sys... (XD-ADR-0066)*](decisions/repos.html.md#XD-ADR-0066)). Run records live on the orphan branch `evidence`.

Inside the domain there is only one kind of link: a test result points to its test case. Everything a system-level test verifies is in SYS (next section). A HIL run result also carries the firmware hash and the pinned SHAs ([*HIL results carry the firmw... (XD-RULE-0012)*](rules/trace.html.md#XD-RULE-0012)).

## Between two domains

These diagrams show only the links that cross from one domain to another. Almost every crossing
link goes to systems engineering: a domain requirement refines a system requirement or an
interface, a design element realizes a system model element and conforms to an interface, and a
system-level test verifies a system requirement or an interface. The one exception is SW to EE
(last diagram below).

Peer domains meet at an interface need (`SYS-IF`) in the systems repo, and both link to it
with `conforms` ([*Peer interfaces live in sys... (XD-RULE-0003)*](rules/trace.html.md#XD-RULE-0003), [*Link-type table and interfa... (XD-ADR-0030)*](decisions/traces.html.md#XD-ADR-0030)). In the diagrams above,
`Interface` is that meeting point. There is one exception: SW reads the board pin maps from
the EE model, so a SW architecture or detailed design element may conform to a PCBA or a
connector port of EE (`ee-pcba`, `ee-port`); EE never links to SW. EE and ME, and ME and
SW, have no links to each other, so they have no diagram.

System-level verification has no links to the engineering domains
either: it verifies the system. It does import their needs, as the next section shows.

## The whole workspace

Needs flow from the abstract side to the concrete side. The systems repo exports its needs. The
engineering repos import them as upstream documents, and the integration repo imports all of them
([*One zdocs registry per repo... (XD-ADR-0021)*](decisions/documents.html.md#XD-ADR-0021)). Trace links point back the other way ([*The concrete side holds the... (XD-RULE-0001)*](rules/trace.html.md#XD-RULE-0001)). The manifest
builds the full set.

xdmod serves every repo with the need types, the Sphinx extensions, the SysML library, curatrix
and the reusable workflows ([*xdmod is in every workspace (XD-ADR-0059)*](decisions/repos.html.md#XD-ADR-0059)). The manifest pins every repo
([*The manifest is the only pl... (XD-ADR-0033)*](decisions/repos.html.md#XD-ADR-0033)).

«keyword» and</text><text x="14" y="711">&lt;short name&gt; above the name; lists inside a box = compartments. Hollow triangle = specialization (points to the general</text><text x="14" y="722">type); filled diamond = composite feature; hollow diamond with open arrow = reference feature, held by the diamond end</text><text x="14" y="733">and labelled with the link type and multiplicity. ":&gt; T" under the name = specializes T, written as in the textual</text><text x="14" y="744">notation when T has no box here. A box with a need ID links to its page.</text></g></g></g><g data-cell-id="XdmodDomains::Repository-&gt;XdmodDomains::AgentMaterial:agentMaterial"><g transform="translate(0.5,0.5)"><path d="M 240.99 371.02 L 235 371 L 235 604 L 371 604" fill="none" stroke="#333333" style="stroke: light-dark(rgb(51, 51, 51), rgb(193, 193, 193));" stroke-miterlimit="10" pointer-events="stroke"/><path d="M 225.99 370.97 L 233.5 366.58 L 240.99 371.02 L 233.47 375.41 Z" fill="#333333" style="fill: light-dark(rgb(51, 51, 51), rgb(193, 193, 193)); «keyword» and</text><text x="674" y="49">&lt;short name&gt; above the name; lists inside a box =</text><text x="674" y="60">compartments. Hollow triangle = specialization (points to</text><text x="674" y="71">the general type); filled diamond = composite feature;</text><text x="674" y="82">hollow diamond with open arrow = reference feature, held by</text><text x="674" y="93">the diamond end and labelled with the link type and</text><text x="674" y="104">multiplicity. ":&gt; T" under the name = specializes T, written</text><text x="674" y="115">as in the textual notation when T has no box here. XDMOD knows three kinds of files. **Source** files are written by people and agents.
**Generated** files are written by curatrix, committed, and compared by `curatrix check`
([*Generated files are committ... (XD-RULE-0033)*](rules/checks.html.md#XD-RULE-0033)). **Build output** goes to `build/<area>/` and is never committed
([*Build output only under build/ (XD-RULE-0040)*](rules/checks.html.md#XD-RULE-0040)). The plain source files (documents, models, configuration) are in the repo
diagrams above.

## Need categories

`Need` declares every link type once. Each need type then narrows the link types it holds to
the target types it may point to ([*Link types are declared on ... (XD-RULE-0002)*](rules/trace.html.md#XD-RULE-0002)). The categories group the need types by
their place in the V.

Test results come from two sources: run results written by curatrix and test reports imported from
JUnit files. Each points to the test case it comes from (domain diagrams above).

Decisions, rules and checks are needs too ([*Decisions become sphinx-needs (XD-ADR-0109)*](decisions/traces.html.md#XD-ADR-0109), [*Method rules are needs (XD-ADR-0110)*](decisions/traces.html.md#XD-ADR-0110)). A rule
is decided by a decision, and a check enforces a rule. A project decision follows a framework
decision.

## Build and check

The same steps run locally and in CI ([*Every check also runs locally (XD-ADR-0040)*](decisions/checks.html.md#XD-ADR-0040), [*Every check runs locally (XD-RULE-0032)*](rules/checks.html.md#XD-RULE-0032)). Each action
has its inputs and outputs as pins, and the thick arrows show which file goes where. Only a
passing check leads to publishing.

## The change process

A change runs on one branch name in every repo it touches ([*One branch name across repos (XD-ADR-0045)*](decisions/workflow.html.md#XD-ADR-0045)). It is checked
locally and in CI, and reviewed by a second agent ([*Every PR gets an agent review (XD-ADR-0115)*](decisions/workflow.html.md#XD-ADR-0115)). Then a person, the owner
or a domain engineer, reviews and merges it ([*Trunk-based merging with re... (XD-ADR-0043)*](decisions/workflow.html.md#XD-ADR-0043), [*Only a human's commit affirms (XD-ADR-0051)*](decisions/workflow.html.md#XD-ADR-0051)); during
Phase 5 the coordinator agent may also merge after an agent review without open must-fix findings. If the
change made links suspect, an agent prepares the affirmations and a person confirms them
([*Suspect links are reviewed ... (XD-RULE-0014)*](rules/trace.html.md#XD-RULE-0014)). After each action name comes its action definition: the kind of task
(agent work, agent review, a person’s decision, a CI run), which says who may do it.

The bump bot runs on a schedule, not as a step of one change ([*The bump bot is a scheduled... (XD-ADR-0081)*](decisions/checks.html.md#XD-ADR-0081)). It collects
the new SHAs of all repos into one bump PR, whose full-set check must pass before the owner merges
it. Then the documents are published.

## Life cycles

A need starts as `draft`. A merged change sets it to `valid`; only `valid` needs count for
coverage. A need is retired before it is deleted, and its ID is never issued again
([*IDs are never reused; retir... (XD-RULE-0020)*](rules/ids.html.md#XD-RULE-0020), [*Links to retired needs are ... (XD-RULE-0011)*](rules/trace.html.md#XD-RULE-0011)).

A newer decision revises or supersedes an older one, and the newer one holds the link
([*Decisions become sphinx-needs (XD-ADR-0109)*](decisions/traces.html.md#XD-ADR-0109)).

A link becomes suspect when its target changes. Only a person’s commit confirms it again
([*Only a human's commit affirms (XD-ADR-0051)*](decisions/workflow.html.md#XD-ADR-0051), [*Suspect links before affirm... (XD-ADR-0121)*](decisions/checks.html.md#XD-ADR-0121)), and no suspect link may remain at a baseline
([*Suspect links are reviewed ... (XD-RULE-0014)*](rules/trace.html.md#XD-RULE-0014)).

HIL evidence counts only when it was recorded from `main` or a baseline, and only while the
pinned SHAs still match ([*Evidence only from main or ... (XD-ADR-0053)*](decisions/checks.html.md#XD-ADR-0053), [*Evidence validity (XD-ADR-0120)*](decisions/checks.html.md#XD-ADR-0120), [*Only valid evidence counts (XD-RULE-0013)*](rules/trace.html.md#XD-RULE-0013)). A record
never changes: a new run writes a new record.

## People, agents and automation

Agents author and review like people do ([*Agents are first-class (XD-ADR-0003)*](decisions/workflow.html.md#XD-ADR-0003)). Only people decide: the owner
merges, and a person affirms suspect links ([*Only a human's commit affirms (XD-ADR-0051)*](decisions/workflow.html.md#XD-ADR-0051)). The full list of what agents
may and may not do is in [Humans and agents](humans-and-agents.html.md).
